Serre Duality and the Whitehead Link
Cailan Li

Abstract
We prove the action of the full twist is a Serre functor in the homotopy category of dihedral Soergel Bimodules. Our proof relies on the representability of a certain partial trace functor, which we prove using the diagrammatics for Hochschild cohomology of Soergel Bimodules. Leveraging these representability results, we then compute the triply-graded link homology of the Whitehead link.



1
Introduction

Categorification has become an essential perspective in modern representation theory and low-dimensional topology. By replacing algebraic structures with richer categorical analogues, one obtains invariants and structures that reflect deeper geometric or homological phenomena.

1.1
History

The study of braid group actions on triangulated categories has a long and well-documented history, bridging together representation theory, topology, and geometry, see
[
ST01
]
,
[
KS02
]
,
[
RZ03
]
. Seeking to categorify such actions, Rouquier
[
Rou04
]
constructs a categorification of the braid group
B_{W}
associated to a Coxeter group
W
, which associates to
\beta\in W
a (Rouquier) complex
F^{\bullet}_{\beta}\in K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W))
in the homotopy category of Soergel bimodules. For
W=S_{n}
, Khovanov
[
Kho07
]
applies the functor of Hochschild cohomology to
F^{\bullet}_{\beta}
, obtaining a complex whose cohomology yields a triply-graded link homology
\overline{\mathrm{HHH}}
categorifying the HOMFLY–PT polynomial.

In
[
K“’al09
]
, Kálmán established a relation between the “top” and “bottom” parts of the HOMFLY–PT polynomial, expressed in terms of the full twist braid
\mathrm{FT}_{n}
on
n
strands. This relationship was subsequently categorified by Gorsky, Hogancamp, Mellit, Nakagane in
[
GHMN19
]
, where the authors proved an analogue of Serre duality for
K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathbb{C}^{n},S_{n}))
. A central step in their argument involved a form of relative Serre duality, which they further conjectured should hold for arbitrary Coxeter groups
(W,S)
and realizations
\mathfrak{h}
. The setting is as follows.

•

R:=\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}(V^{*}(-2))
and
R^{e}=R\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}R
.
R^{e}-\mathrm{gmod}
are the same as graded
R
bimodules.

•

\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W)
denotes the full subcategory of
R^{e}-\mathrm{gmod}
defined in
[
EW16
, Section 3.4]
consisting of the Soergel Bimodules for
W
associated to the realization
\mathfrak{h}
. It is monoidally generated by
B_{s},\ s\in S
.

•

Given a subset
I\subset S
, the full, graded, monoidal, idempotent complete subcategory of
\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W)
generated by
B_{s},s\in I
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W_{I})
.

•

\mathrm{FT}_{S}:=F^{\bullet}_{w_{0}}\otimes_{R}F^{\bullet}_{w_{0}}
where
F^{\bullet}_{w_{0}}
is the Rouquier complex associated to the longest element in
W
.
\mathrm{FT}_{S/I}:=\mathrm{FT}_{S}\otimes_{R}\mathrm{FT}_{I}^{-1}\cong\mathrm{FT}_{I}^{-1}\otimes_{R}\mathrm{FT}_{S}
.

Conjecture 1

(Relative Serre Duality)
.

Given a realization
\mathfrak{h}
of
W
, let
\pi_{L},\pi_{R}:K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W))\to K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W_{I}))
be the left and right adjoints to the inclusion functor
K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W_{I}))\to K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W))
. Then

\pi_{R}(X^{\bullet})\simeq\pi_{L}(\mathrm{FT}_{S/I}\otimes_{R}X^{\bullet})\in K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W_{I}))

for all
X^{\bullet}\in K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W))
.

In the case
W=S_{n}
, with
\mathfrak{h}=\mathbb{C}^{n}
and
W_{I}=S_{r}\times(S_{1})^{n-r}
, this conjecture was established in
[
GHMN19
]
. For Weyl Groups and their associated Cartan realizations, this was recently shown by Ho and Li in
[
HL25
]
.

1.2
Main Results

In this paper we prove
1
for
W=I_{2}(m)=\left\langle s,t|\,s^{2}=t^{2}=(st)^{m}=e\right\rangle
, the dihedral group, for any symmetric realization of
I_{2}(m)
satisfying the non-degeneracy conditions stated in
1
and
2
. This furnishes the first non-crystallographic instance of the phenomenon. As a consequence, we show that tensoring with the complex
\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}}
defines a Serre functor on
K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m)))
; namely

Theorem 1.1

(Serre Duality)
.

For any two complexes
X^{\bullet},Y^{\bullet}\in K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m)))
one has the following homotopy equivalences of complexes right (or left)
R-
modules.

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R^{e}}(X^{\bullet},Y^{\bullet})\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R^{e}}(Y^{\bullet},\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}}^{-1}\otimes_{R}X^{\bullet})^{\vee}\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R^{e}}(\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}}\otimes_{R}Y^{\bullet},X^{\bullet})^{\vee}

where
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R^{e}}(-,-)
is the complex of Homs and
\vee=\mathbb{D}
1
1

1

This equality holds for
R-
modules, but not for arbitrary
R-
bimodules.
is the
R-
linear dual, see
Definition

2.3
.

Remark
.

Serre functors for
\mathbbm{k}-
linear categories
\mathcal{C}
typically use the
\mathbbm{k}-
linear dual
(-)^{*}=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbbm{k}}(-,\mathbbm{k})
but this requires that the
\mathrm{Hom}
spaces in
\mathcal{C}
are finite
\mathbbm{k}
modules which is not the case for Soergel Bimodules.

Remark
.

The category of Soergel modules
\mathbb{S}\mathrm{Mod}(\mathfrak{h},W)
is obtained from
\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W)
by quotienting out the right (or left) action of
R
. Its objects are of the form

\overline{M}=M\otimes_{R}R/R^{+}\cong M\otimes_{R}\mathbbm{k}\qquad M\in\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W)

When
W
is a Weyl group with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathrm{Cart}}
denotes the Cartan realization, Soergel
[
Soe90
]
established the following equivalence of categories

D^{b}(\mathcal{O}_{0})\cong K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\mathrm{Mod}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathrm{Cart}},W))

where
\mathcal{O}_{0}
is the principal block of Category
\mathcal{O}
for
\mathfrak{g}
. In this setting,
\mathrm{Hom}
spaces in are finite
\mathbbm{k}
modules and it was shown in
[
BBM04
]
and
[
MS08
]
that the Serre functor for
D^{b}(\mathcal{O}_{0})
is given by the action of the full twist.

Thus, applying the functor
\otimes_{R}\mathbbm{k}
to
Theorem

1.1
, we obtain a version of Serre duality for dihedral Category
\mathcal{O}

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{C}(\overline{X^{\bullet}},\overline{Y^{\bullet}})=\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{C}(\overline{Y^{\bullet}},\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}}^{-1}\otimes_{R}\overline{X^{\bullet}})^{*}\qquad\forall\overline{X^{\bullet}},\overline{Y^{\bullet}}\in K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\mathrm{Mod}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m)))

where
C=R/(R^{W}_{+})
is the coinvariant algebra. See
[
GHMN19
, Section 6.4]
for more details.

A key step in our proof of
1
for dihedral groups is the explicit computation of the left and right adjoints to the inclusion functor
\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},\left\langle t\right\rangle)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m))
on objects in
\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m))
. In particular, using tools from the diagrammatics of Ext groups between Soergel bimodules developed in
[
Li25a
]
, we show that

Theorem 1.2
.

Let
w\in I_{2}(m)
and let
B_{w}\in\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m))
be the corresponding indecomposable bimodule. Then

(a)

\pi^{\pm}_{s}(R)\cong R
.

(b)

\pi^{\pm}_{s}(B_{s})\cong R(\pm 1)
.

(c)

If
w
contains at least one
t
,
\pi_{s}^{-}(B_{w})\cong B_{t}(-\ell(w)+1)
and
\pi_{s}^{+}(B_{w})\cong B_{t}(\ell(w)-1)
.

where
\pi^{-}_{s}(M),\pi^{+}_{s}(M)
are defined in
Definition

3.1
and shown to be the right and left adjoints in
Corollary

3.4
. Unlike the situation in type
A
, where
[
GHMN19
]
bypassed such a calculation by appealing to special structural features, the dihedral case requires this explicit analysis. (see
Section

5
for more details). Moreover, for
m=3
, this gives explicit descriptions of the partial Hochschild cohomology functors used in the construction of triply-graded link homology. As a consequence, we determine the triply graded link homology of the Whitehead link (see
Fig.

1
):

Theorem 1.3
.

For the braid representative
\sigma_{1}^{-2}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{1}^{-1}\sigma_{2}
of the Whitehead link,the Poincare series for
\overline{\mathrm{HHH}}
will be

\displaystyle\overline{\mathscr{P}(A,Q,T)}

\displaystyle=TQ^{-1}+T^{2}Q^{-3}+A\left(T^{-1}Q^{-1}+Q^{-3}+\frac{Q^{-3}}{(1-Q^{2})}+TQ^{-5}+T^{2}Q^{-7}\right)+A^{2}\left(T^{-1}Q^{-5}+\frac{Q^{-7}}{(1-Q^{2})}\right)

While computations in triply-graded link homology are generally formidable, the case of positive and negative braid links admits more accessible calculations. For example,
\overline{\mathrm{HHH}}
of positive torus links
T(m,n)
with
m,n\geq 0
was determined in
[
EH19
]
,
[
HM19
]
, and partial results for all positive and negative braid links were obtained in
[
Li25
]
. In contrast, our work provides the first genuinely nontrivial computations of triply-graded link homology in the setting of mixed braid links.

Figure 1:
A planar diagram of the Whitehead link.

Outline

•

In

we recall the definition of a symmetric realization
\mathfrak{h}
of a Coxeter system and review the tools needed from
[

and

.

.

contains the proof of Relative Serre Duality, adapting the approach developed in

GHMN19
]
. Our proof of Serre duality differs slightly, as

, Lemma 3.9]
is not correct as written. We provide an alternative argument, from which the main results of
[
GHMN19
]
still follow.

•

In

we conjecture an explicit description of the adjoints of the inclusion functor for general parabolic subgroups of Coxeter groups, and lightly sketch how
1
could be deduced from one or two central statements.

•

In

, we compute the triply-graded link homology of the Whitehead link (Rolfsen
L5a1
) using tools from
Section

3
. This section may be read independently of
Sections

4
and
5
.

2
Preliminaries

Definition 2.1
.

Let
\mathbbm{k}
be a commutative ring. A symmetric realization of
(W,S)
over
\mathbbm{k}
is a triple

\mathfrak{h}=(V,\left\{\alpha_{s}\right\}_{s\in S}\subset V^{*},\left\{\alpha_{s}^{\vee}\right\}_{s\in S}\subset V)

where
V
is a free, finite rank
\mathbbm{k}
module such that for
\left\langle-,-\right\rangle
the natural pairing between
V,V^{*}
,

(1)

\left\langle\alpha_{s}^{\vee},\alpha_{s}\right\rangle=2
for all
s\in S
.

(2)

a_{st}=a_{ts}
.

(3)

The assignment
s(v)=v-\left\langle v,\alpha_{s}\right\rangle\alpha_{s}^{\vee}
for all
v\in V
yields a representation of
W
.

(4)

For each
s,t\in S
let
a_{st}:=\left\langle\alpha_{s}^{\vee},\alpha_{t}\right\rangle
and
m_{st}
the order of
st
in
W
. Write
a_{st}=-(q+q^{-1})
for
q\in\mathbbm{k}
. Then
[m_{st}]_{q}=0
where
[k]_{q}
are the quantum numbers defined in Section 3.1 of
[
Eli16
]
.

Definition 2.2
.

A realization
\mathfrak{h}
has fundamental weights if
\forall s\in S
,
\exists\rho_{s}\in V^{*}
s.t.
\left\langle\rho_{s},\alpha_{t}^{\vee}\right\rangle=\delta_{st}
for all coroots
\alpha_{t}^{\vee}
.

It is clear from the definition that fundamental weights for
\mathfrak{h}
exists
\iff\left\{\alpha_{s}^{\vee}\right\}
are linearly independent. Throughout the remainder of the paper, until
Section

5
, we will work with the Coxeter system
(W=I_{2}(m),S=\left\{s,t\right\})
and assume that our realization
\mathfrak{h}
of
I_{2}(m)
satisfies the following

Assumption 1
.

\mathfrak{h}
has fundamental weights,
\mathrm{char}\ \mathbbm{k}=0
, and
\left\{\alpha_{s},\alpha_{t}\right\}
is linearly independent.

Assumption 2
.

For all
k<m
,
[k]
is invertible over
\mathbbm{k}
and
q
is a primitive
2m
-th root of unity.

These assumptions are needed so that the results of
[
Li25a
]
hold.

2.1
Notation

•

\boxed{M}
around an object
M
indicates that
M
is placed in cohomological degree 0.

•

(1)
shifts the internal grading down by 1, e.g.
M(1)_{d}=M_{d+1}
.

•

\mathrm{BS}(\underline{w})=B_{s_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes B_{s_{k}}
for an expression
\underline{w}=(s_{1}\ldots,s_{k})
,
s_{i}\in S
.

•

B_{w}
is the indecomposable Soergel Bimodule corresponding to
w\in W
.

•

Given an
R-
bimodule
M
,
\mathrm{HH}^{k}(M)=\mathrm{Ext}_{R^{e}}^{k}(R,M)
and
\mathrm{HH}_{k}(M)=\mathrm{Tor}^{R^{e}}_{k}(R,M)
.

•

\left\langle X_{i}^{\bullet}\right\rangle_{i\in I}=
the span of
\left\{X_{i}^{\bullet}\right\}_{i\in I}
which is the smallest graded triangulated subcategory containing the complexes
\left\{X_{i}^{\bullet}\right\}_{i\in I}
. Equivalently, close under taking
(1)
and
[1]
shifts, and taking cones and direct sums.

•

\mathrm{Tot}
is the total complex of a double complex.

2.2
Review of
[
Li25a
]

Definition 2.3
.

Let
\mathbb{D}:R^{e}-\mathrm{gmod}\to R^{e}-\mathrm{gmod}
be the functor

\mathbb{D}(N):=\mathrm{Hom}_{\_R}(N,R)

where
\_R
means we take right
R
module homomorphisms. This has a
R^{e}-\mathrm{gmod}
structure defined as
(r\cdot f\cdot r^{\prime})(b):=r\cdot f(b)\cdot r^{\prime}
.

Lemma 2.4

(
[
Li25a
, Theorem 7.2]
,
[
Li25a
, Theorem 3.12]
)
.

Let
\rho_{s}^{e}:=\rho_{s}\otimes 1-1\otimes\rho_{s}\in R^{e}
. If
1
and
2
holds for the realization
\mathfrak{h}
, then we have isomorphism of bimodules

\ker\rho_{s}^{e}(\mathrm{BS}(\underline{w}))\overset{R^{e}}{\cong}\mathrm{Hom}_{R^{e}}(B_{t},\mathrm{BS}(\underline{w}))(1)\qquad\mathrm{coker}\rho_{s}^{e}(\mathrm{BS}(\underline{w}))\overset{R^{e}}{\cong}\mathbb{D}(\mathrm{Hom}_{R^{e}}(B_{t},\mathrm{BS}(\underline{w})(1))\qquad\forall\underline{w}\neq\emptyset

Warning
.

It is tempting to use the adjunction
\mathrm{Hom}_{R^{e}}(B_{t},\mathrm{BS}(\underline{w}))\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{R^{e}}(R,B_{t}\otimes_{R}\mathrm{BS}(\underline{w}))
above but this adjunction is only an isomorphism of right
R-
modules, not as bimodules.

Lemma 2.5
.

Given
B\in\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m))
, if
1
and
2
holds for the realization
\mathfrak{h}
, then
\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{R^{e}}(R,B)
is a free
R-
module for all
k
.

Proof.

[
Li25a
, Theorem 7.12]
shows this for the geometric realization and
[
Li25a
, Theorem 3.6]
shows that
\mathrm{Ext}
for other realizations can be gotten by tensoring over
\mathbbm{k}
with a finite free module over
\mathbbm{k}
.
∎

2.3
Review of
[
GHMN19
]

In this subsection, we collect some definitions and results from
[
GHMN19
]
that are used later in this paper.

Definition 2.6
.

Suppose
\mathcal{A}
is a triangulated category that has a monoidal structure
\otimes
. Then
\mathcal{A}
is a triangulated monoidal category with monoidal unit
\mathbbm{1}
if
a\otimes-
and
-\otimes a
are triangulated functors for any
a\in\mathcal{A}
.

Definition 2.7
.

A
unital idempotent
in
\mathcal{A}
is a pair
(\mathbf{Q},\eta)
where
\mathbf{Q}\in\mathcal{A}
and
\eta:\mathbbm{1}\to\mathbf{Q}
, s.t.
\eta\otimes 1_{\mathbf{Q}},1_{\mathbf{Q}}\otimes\eta:\mathbf{Q}\to\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{Q}
are isomorphisms. A
counital idempotent
in
\mathcal{A}
is a pair
(\mathbf{P},\epsilon)
where
\mathbf{P}\in\mathcal{A}
and
\eta:\mathbf{P}\to\mathbbm{1}
, s.t.
\epsilon\otimes 1_{\mathbf{P}},1_{\mathbf{P}}\otimes\epsilon:\mathbf{P}\mathbf{P}\to\mathbf{P}
are isomorphisms.

Definition 2.8
.

Given a triangulated monoidal category
\mathcal{A}
, an
A-
module category
\mathcal{M}
is a triangulated category such that the action of
\mathcal{A}
on
\mathcal{M}
is triangulated.

Example 1
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is an additive category, then
\mathcal{A}=K^{b}(\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{C}))
is a triangulated monoidal category where
\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{C})
is the category of endofunctors of
\mathcal{C}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{M}=K^{b}(\mathcal{C})
is an
\mathcal{A}-
module category, where given
M^{\bullet}\in K^{b}(\mathcal{C})
and
F=[0\to\boxed{F}\to 0]\in\mathcal{A}
the action of the endofunctor
F
on
M^{\bullet}
is given by

F\cdot M^{\bullet}=[\ldots\to F(M^{i})\xrightarrow{F(d)}F(M^{i+1})\to\ldots]

For general
F^{\bullet}\in\mathcal{A}
, we take the total complex for
F^{\bullet}\cdot M^{\bullet}
.

Definition 2.9

(Two-Step)
.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}\subset\mathcal{M}
be full triangulated subcategories. Then
(\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2})
is a a semi-orthogonal decomposition of
\mathcal{M}
, written
\mathcal{M}\simeq(\mathcal{M}_{1}\to\mathcal{M}_{2})
if

(1)

Each object
X
of
\mathcal{M}
fits into a distinguished triangle

X_{2}\to X\to X_{1}\xrightarrow{+1}\qquad X_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{i}

(2)

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathcal{M}_{2},\mathcal{M}_{1})=0

Lemma 2.10

(
[
GHMN19
, Corollary 4.14]
)
.

If
\mathbf{P}
(resp.
\mathbf{Q}
) is a counital (resp. unital) idempotent in
\mathcal{A}
then each
\mathcal{A}
-module category
\mathcal{M}
inherits a semi-orthogonal decomposition
\mathcal{M}\simeq(\ker\mathbf{P}\to\mathrm{im}\,\mathbf{P})
(resp.
\mathcal{M}\simeq(\mathrm{im}\,\mathbf{Q}\to\ker\mathbf{Q})
).

Lemma 2.11

(
[
GHMN19
, Lemma 4.17]
)
.

Let
\{X_{i}\}_{i\in I}
and
\{Y_{j}\}_{j\in J}
be a collection of objects in
\mathcal{M}
. Let
\mathbf{E}
be a unital or counital idempotent in
\mathcal{A}
. Assume that
\mathbf{E}(X_{i})\simeq 0
for all
i
and
\mathbf{E}(Y_{j})\simeq Y_{j}
for all
j
. Then:

(3)

If
\{X_{i}\}_{i\in I}
and
\{Y_{j}\}_{j\in J}
together span all of
\mathcal{M}
as a triangulated category, then
\{X_{i}\}_{i\in I}
spans the kernel of
\,\mathbf{E}
while
\{Y_{j}\}_{j\in J}
spans the image of
\,\mathbf{E}
.

3
Partial Trace

Definition 3.1
.

Given
s\in S
,
M\in\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m))
, let
\pi^{-}_{s}
,
\pi^{+}_{s}
be the kernel, cokernel, respectively, of the action of
\rho_{s}^{e}=\rho_{s}\otimes 1-1\otimes\rho_{s}
on
M
.

0\to\pi^{-}_{s}(M)\to M\xrightarrow{\rho_{s}\otimes 1-1\otimes\rho_{s}}M\to\pi^{+}_{s}(M)\to 0

Remark
.

Let
\eta
be the inclusion map
\pi^{-}_{s}(M)\to M
above and
\epsilon
the projection map
M\to\pi^{+}_{s}(M)
. Then
(\pi^{-}_{s},\eta)
,
(\pi^{+}_{s},\epsilon)
are counital/unital idempotents in
K^{b}(\mathrm{End}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m))))
respectively.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
J
be the projector to a sub-bimodule
N\subset M
and let
T:M\to M
be a bimodule endomorphism. If
TJ=JT
then
\ker(T|_{N})=J(\ker T)
.

Proof.

We always have
\ker(T|_{N})\subset J(\ker T)
and
TJ=JT
implies
J(\ker T)\subset\ker(T|_{N})
.
∎

Theorem 3.3
.

Let
w\in I_{2}(m)
and
B_{w}\in\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m))
. Then

(a)

\pi^{\pm}_{s}(R)\cong R
.

(b)

\pi^{\pm}_{s}(B_{s})\cong R(\pm 1)
.

(c)

If
w
contains at least one
t
,
\pi_{s}^{-}(B_{w})\cong B_{t}(-\ell(w)+1)
and
\pi_{s}^{+}(B_{w})\cong B_{t}(\ell(w)-1)
.

Proof.

Let
\underline{w}
be a reduced expression. From
Lemma

2.4
we have that

\pi^{-}_{s}(\mathrm{BS}(\underline{w}))\overset{R^{e}}{\cong}\mathrm{Hom}_{R^{e}}(B_{t},\mathrm{BS}(\underline{w}))(1)

(1)

Let
\mathrm{JW}_{\underline{w}}
be the projector to the indecomposable
B_{w}
in
\mathrm{BS}(\underline{w})
. It is clear diagrammatically that
\mathrm{JW}_{\underline{w}}
commutes with
\rho_{s}^{e}
and thus

\pi_{s}^{-}(B_{w})=\ker\rho_{s}^{e}(\underline{w})|_{B_{w}}\overset{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:jker}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}\mathrm{JW}_{\underline{w}}\left(\ker\rho_{s}^{e}(\underline{w})\right)\overset{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq1}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}\mathrm{JW}_{\underline{w}}\left(\mathrm{Hom}_{R^{e}}(B_{t},\mathrm{BS}(\underline{w}))(1)\right)=\mathrm{Hom}_{R^{e}}(B_{t},B_{w})(1)

(2)

(b)
Using
Eq.

2
and the Double Leaves basis
[
EMTW20

, we see that

(3)

, Proposition 18.9]
then gives the analogous results for

Corollary 3.4
.

\pi^{-}_{s},\pi^{+}_{s}
are the right and left adjoints to the inclusion
\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},\left\langle t\right\rangle)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m))
.

Proof.

Similar to the proof of
[
GHMN19
, Proposition 5.11]
2
2

2

There is a typo in the proof,
\pi^{-}
is a co-localization functor,
\pi^{+}
is a localization functor.
,
\pi^{-}_{s},\pi^{+}_{s}
are co-localization and localization functors so by
[
GHMN19
, Lemma 4.4]
it suffices to show the essential image of
\pi^{-}_{s},\pi^{+}_{s}
is
\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},\left\langle t\right\rangle)
. This follows from the above lemma.
∎

Corollary 3.5
.

\pi_{s}^{-}:\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m))\to\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},\left\langle t\right\rangle)
is represented by the object
B_{t}(-1)
.

Proof.

Direct consequence of
Eq.

2
.
∎

Remark
.

Because both
\pi^{-}_{s}
,
\pi^{+}_{s}
are additive, they descend to triangulated functors
K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m)))\to K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},\left\langle t\right\rangle))
.

Lemma 3.6
.

\pi^{\pm}_{s}
is
\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},\left\langle t\right\rangle)-
bilinear, meaning

\pi^{\pm}_{s}(M\otimes_{R}X\otimes_{R}N)=M\otimes_{R}\pi^{\pm}_{s}(X)\otimes_{R}N

for all
M,N\in\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},\left\langle t\right\rangle)
and
X\in\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m))
.

Proof.

This is because
\left\langle\rho_{s},\alpha_{t}^{\vee}\right\rangle=0
so
\rho_{s}
slides past
M,N
.
∎

Lemma 3.7
.

In
I_{2}(m)
,
\displaystyle\delta_{w}=\sum_{y\leq w}(-v)^{\ell(w)-\ell(y)}b_{y}
.

Proof.

It is well known that for the dihedral group, “all KL polynomials are 1” meaning that for
w\in I_{2}(m)

b_{w}=\sum_{y\leq w}v^{\ell(w)-\ell(y)}\delta_{y}\implies v^{-\ell(w)}b_{w}=\sum_{y\leq w}v^{-\ell(y)}\delta_{y}

The RHS above is suitable for Mobius inversion. Because the Mobius function for the Bruhat poset of a Coxeter system is of the form
\mu(y,w)=(-1)^{\ell(w)-\ell(y)}

[
Ste07
]
it follows that

v^{-\ell(w)}\delta_{w}=\sum_{y\leq w}\mu(y,w)v^{-\ell(y)}b_{y}\implies\delta_{w}=\sum_{y\leq w}(-v)^{\ell(w)-\ell(y)}b_{y}

∎

Proposition 3.8
.

The minimal complex of
F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{k}}
in
K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m)))
when
1\leq k\leq\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\rfloor
is of the form (boxed term is in cohomological degree 0)

\boxed{B_{(st)^{k}}}\xrightarrow{}B_{(st)^{k-1}s}(1)\oplus B_{(ts)^{k-1}t}(1)\xrightarrow{}B_{(st)^{k-1}}(2)\oplus B_{(ts)^{k-1}}(2)\to\ldots\to R(2k)

(4)

where the differentials are uniquely determined up to a scalar. Note that besides the first and last cohomological degrees, the rest have exactly two summands in them. A similar result holds for
F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{k}s}
.

Proof.

(st)^{k}
is a reduced expression in
I_{2}(m)
for
k\leq\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\rfloor
and thus by
[
EMTW20
, Theorem 19.47]

F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{k}}
is perverse, meaning that objects with internal degree shifts
(n)
in the minimal complex for
F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{k}}
are in cohomological degree
n
. It follows that the class of
F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{k}}
in the Grothendieck group determines the terms for the minimal complex for
F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{k}}
so by
Lemma

3.7
we obtain
Eq.

4
, modulo the differentials. However, for
w,u\in I_{2}(m)
s.t.
\ell(w)=\ell(u)+1
, we claim

\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}\mathrm{Hom}^{1}(B_{w},B_{u})=1

This will follow from Soergel’s Hom formula as we compute that

\epsilon(b_{w}b_{u})=\sum_{x\leq w,\,y\leq u}v^{\ell(w)-\ell(x)+\ell(u)-\ell(y)}\epsilon(\delta_{x}\delta_{y})

Using
[
EMTW20
, Lemma 3.15]
we have
x=y^{-1}
, and thus the RHS equals
\sum_{y\leq v}v^{2\ell(u)-2\ell(y)+1}
and it follows the coefficient of
v^{1}
in
\epsilon(b_{w}b_{u})
is 1 as desired.
∎

Remark
.

\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}\mathrm{Hom}^{1}(B_{w},B_{v})=1
when
v<w
and
\ell(w)=\ell(v)+1
actually holds in any Coxeter group. Using
[
EMTW20
, Lemma 3.26]
, it follows that

b_{w}=\delta_{w}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell(w)=\ell(y)+1\\
y<w\end{subarray}}v\delta_{y}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell(w)>\ell(z)+1\\
z<w\end{subarray}}h_{z,w}\delta_{z},\qquad h_{z,w}\in v\mathbb{Z}[v]

Thus when computing
\epsilon(b_{w}b_{u})
,
[
EMTW20
, Lemma 3.15]
again says we must pair
x
with its inverse and so only
\delta_{u}
, the leading term of
b_{u}
can contribute a factor of
v
.

Corollary 3.9
.

(i)

For
1\leq k\leq\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\rfloor
,
\pi^{+}_{s}\left(F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{k}}\right)\simeq\pi^{+}_{s}\left(F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{k}s}\right)\simeq 0
in
\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},\left\langle t\right\rangle)
.

(ii)

\pi^{+}_{s}\left(F_{s}\right)\simeq 0

Proof.

(i)
It suffices to show
\pi^{+}_{s}\left(F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{k}}\right)\simeq 0
as the other case proceeds similarly. Applying
\pi^{+}_{s}
to
Eq.

4
and using
Theorem

3.3
,
(c)
we obtain

\pi^{+}_{s}\left(F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{k}}\right)=\boxed{B_{t}(2k-1)}\to B_{t}(2k-1)\oplus B_{t}(2k-1)\to\ldots\to B_{t}(2k-1)\oplus R(2k)\to R(2k)

As
\mathrm{Hom}_{R^{e}}(B_{t},B_{t})\cong R\oplus R(-2)
, the only degree 0 morphisms are isomorphisms and thus every component of the differentials above are isomorphisms except in the last 3 terms on the right. We can then use Gaussian elimination in the homotopy category starting from the leftmost term.

\pi^{+}_{s}\left(F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{k}}\right)\simeq\boxed{\xcancel{B_{t}(2k-1)}}\to\xcancel{B_{t}(2k-1)}\oplus B_{t}(2k-1)\to\ldots\to B_{t}(2k-1)\oplus R(2k)\to R(2k)

This continues all the way to the right where we end up canceling
R(2k)\to R(2k)
.
(ii)
proceeds similarly.
∎

Corollary 3.10
.

(i)

For
1\leq k\leq\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\rfloor
,
\pi^{-}_{s}\left(F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{-k}}\right)\simeq\pi^{-}_{s}\left(F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{-k}s^{-1}}\right)\simeq 0
in
\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},\left\langle t\right\rangle)
.

(ii)

\pi^{-}_{s}\left(F_{s^{-1}}\right)\simeq 0

Proof.

Same proof as above, but where we use that the minimal complex for
F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{-k}}
is obtained from
F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{k}}
by flipping all arrows and reversing the internal gradings. Concretely,

F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{-k}}\simeq R(-2k)\to\ldots\xrightarrow{}B_{(st)^{k-1}s}(-1)\oplus B_{(ts)^{k-1}t}(-1)\xrightarrow{}\boxed{B_{(st)^{k}}}

(5)

∎

4
Serre Duality

Similar to
[
GHMN19
, Section 6]
, a key role in our proof will be played by ‘‘link-splitting” maps
3
3

3

The name comes from the fact that any link can be unlinked by appropriately switching positive and negative crossings.
. The Rouquier complex
F_{s}
has a chain map
\psi_{s}
to
F_{s}^{-1}
as seen below.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{s}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\boxed{B_{s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R(1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{s}^{-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R(-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\boxed{B_{s}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

After Gaussian elimination,
\mathrm{Cone}(\psi_{s})\simeq R(-1)\xrightarrow{\alpha_{s}}\boxed{R(1)}
and so we have the following distinguished triangle

F_{s}^{2}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}_{F_{s}}\otimes_{R}\psi_{s}}R\to\mathrm{Tot}\left(F_{s}(-1)\xrightarrow{\alpha_{s}}\boxed{F_{s}(1)}\right)\xrightarrow{+1}

(6)

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
\overbrace{F_{s}F_{t}\ldots}^{k}
be the Rouquier complex where we alternate between
F_{s}
and
F_{t}
for
k
terms.

(i)

\pi_{s}^{+}(\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{t}}^{a}F_{s}^{2}\overbrace{F_{t}\ldots}^{b})\simeq\pi_{s}^{+}(\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{s}}^{c}F_{t}^{2}\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots}^{d})\simeq 0
for
|a-b|\geq 2
and
|c-d|\geq 2
and
a+b\leq 2m-3

(ii)

\pi_{s}^{+}(\overbrace{F_{s}F_{t}\ldots}^{k})\simeq 0
for all
2\leq k\leq 2m-3
.

Proof.

Suppose that
(ii)
is true up to
k=k_{0}
. As we are in a triangulated monoidal category, from
Eq.

6
we obtain the distinguished triangle

\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{t}}^{a}F_{s}^{2}\overbrace{F_{t}\ldots}^{b}\to\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{s}}^{a-1}F_{t}^{2}\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots}^{b-1}\to\mathrm{Tot}\left(\overbrace{F_{s}F_{t}\ldots}^{a+b+1}(-1)\to\overbrace{F_{s}F_{t}\ldots}^{a+b+1}(1)\right)

If
a+b\leq k_{0}-1
, then applying
\pi_{s}^{+}
the last term will be 0 by assumption. Repeating this, we obtain the chain of homotopy equivalences

\pi_{s}^{+}\left(\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{t}}^{a}F_{s}^{2}\overbrace{F_{t}\ldots}^{b}\right)\simeq\pi_{s}^{+}\left(\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{s}}^{a-1}F_{t}^{2}\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots}^{b-1}\right)\simeq\ldots\pi_{s}^{+}\left(\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots}^{|a-b|}\right)

As
2\leq|a-b|\leq k_{0}
the last term is homotopy equivalent to 0. Thus,
(ii)
up to
k_{0}\implies(i)
up to
a+b\leq k_{0}-1
.

Now suppose that
(i)
is true up to
a+b\leq k_{0}-1
. Note
(ii)
is already true for
k\leq m
by
Corollary

3.9
. When
k_{0}+1>m
we can apply a braid relation to see that when
m
is odd that

\overbrace{F_{s}F_{t}\ldots F_{s}}^{k_{0}+1}=\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{s}}^{m}\overbrace{F_{t}\ldots}^{k_{0}+1-m}\overset{braid}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}\overbrace{F_{t}\ldots F_{s}}^{m-1}F_{t}^{2}\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{t}}^{k_{0}-m}

If
m
is even then we have
F_{s}^{2}
instead of
F_{t}^{2}
in the middle. Since
m-1+(k_{0}-m)=k_{0}-1
and
|k_{0}-m-(m-1)|=|k_{0}-2m+1|\geq 2
as
k_{0}\leq 2m-3
,
(i)
up to
a+b\leq k_{0}-1
shows the RHS above is homotopy equivalent 0. Thus,
(i)
up to
a+b\leq k_{0}-1
implies
(ii)
up to
k_{0}+1
.
∎

4.1
Semi-Orthogonal Decomposition

Lemma 4.2
.

Given
w\in I_{2}(m)
, let
F_{w}
and
F_{w}^{-1}
be the Rouquier complexes for the positive/negative braid lifts of a chosen reduced expression for
w
. Then
\left\{F_{w}\right\}_{w\in I_{2}(m)}
or
\left\{F_{w}^{-1}\right\}_{w\in I_{2}(m)}
spans
K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m)))
.

Proof.

The indecomposable bimodules
\left\{B_{w}\right\}_{w\in I_{2}(m)}
clearly span
K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m)))
. Taking the cone of the map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R(1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R(1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{s}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\boxed{B_{s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R(1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

(7)

shows
B_{s}
(and
B_{t}
) are in the span of
\left\{F_{w}\right\}_{w\in I_{2}(m)}
. Now proceed by induction on the length of
w
. Using
Eq.

4
we have an inclusion map of complexes
F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{k-1}s}(1)[-1]\to F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{k}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{k-1}s}(1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{(st)^{k-1}s}(1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\boxed{B_{(st)^{k}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{(ts)^{k-1}t}(1)\oplus B_{(st)^{k-1}s}(1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

The cone is isomorphic to the two term complex
\left[\boxed{B_{(st)^{k}}}\to B_{(ts)^{k-1}t}(1)\right]
.
B_{(ts)^{k-1}t}
is in the span of
\left\{F_{w}\right\}_{w\in I_{2}(m)}
by induction so taking the cone of the inclusion map (similar to
Eq.

7
) shows
B_{(st)^{k}}
is also in the span.

The proof for
\left\{F_{w}^{-1}\right\}_{w\in I_{2}(m)}
spanning proceeds similarly where we use
Eq.

5
instead.
∎

Corollary 4.3
.

Let
\mathcal{U}_{t}^{\pm}
be the full triangulated subcategories spanned by the Rouquier complexes
\left\{F_{w}\right\}_{e,t\neq w\in I_{2}(m)}
and
\left\{F_{w}^{-1}\right\}_{e,t\neq w\in I_{2}(m)}
, respectively. Then we have semi-orthogonal decompositions

\displaystyle K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m)))

\displaystyle\simeq\left(\mathcal{U}_{t}^{-}\to K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},\left\langle t\right\rangle))\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\left(K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},\left\langle t\right\rangle))\to\mathcal{U}_{t}^{+}\right)

Proof.

\mathbf{P}=\pi^{-}_{s}
is a counital idempotent and
Theorem

3.3
shows that
\pi^{-}_{s}(R)=R,\pi^{-}_{s}(B_{t})=B_{t}
and thus
\pi^{-}_{s}(F_{t})=F_{t}
.
Corollary

3.10
shows that
\pi^{-}_{s}(F^{\bullet}_{w})\simeq 0
for
e,t\neq w\in I_{2}(m)
.
Lemma

2.11
then implies
that

\ker\pi^{-}_{s}=\mathcal{U}^{-}_{t},\qquad\mathrm{im}\,\pi^{-}_{s}=K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},\left\langle t\right\rangle))

(8)

The first semi-orthogonal decomposition now follows from
Lemma

2.10
. Similarly,
\mathbf{Q}=\pi^{+}_{s}
is a unital idempotent and
Theorem

3.3
shows
\pi^{+}_{s}(F_{t})=F_{t}
while
Corollary

3.10
shows that
\pi^{+}_{s}(F^{\bullet}_{w})\simeq 0
for
e,t\neq w\in I_{2}(m)
so
Lemma

2.11
implies that

\ker\pi^{+}_{s}=\mathcal{U}^{+}_{t},\qquad\mathrm{im}\,\pi^{+}_{s}=K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},\left\langle t\right\rangle))

(9)

and the second semi-orthogonal decomposition also follows from
Lemma

2.10
.
∎

Lemma 4.4
.

Tensoring on the left (or right) with
\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}
(see
Eq.

10
below) gives an equivalence of categories
\mathcal{U}_{t}^{-}\to\mathcal{U}_{t}^{+}
with inverse
\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}^{-1}
.

Proof.

We first show that tensoring on the left with
\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}
sends
\mathcal{U}_{t}^{-}
to
\mathcal{U}_{t}^{+}
by showing it on the generators of
\mathcal{U}_{t}^{-}
. When
m
is odd, we have

\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\overbrace{F_{s}^{-1}F_{t}^{-1}\ldots}^{k}=\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{s}}^{m-2}F_{t}^{2}\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{s}}^{m-2-k}\implies\text{Applying }\pi_{s}^{+}\simeq 0\qquad k\leq m-2

\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\overbrace{F_{s}^{-1}F_{t}^{-1}\ldots}^{m-1}=\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{t}}^{m-1}\implies\text{Applying }\pi_{s}^{+}\simeq 0

\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\overbrace{F_{s}^{-1}F_{t}^{-1}\ldots}^{m}=\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{t}}^{m-1}F_{s}^{-1}\overset{braid}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}F_{t}^{-1}\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{s}}^{m-2}F_{t}\implies\text{Applying }\pi_{s}^{+}\simeq 0

where we have used
4.1
in the last steps above. One can check at the level of the braid group that
\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}t^{-1}=t^{-1}\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}
and thus if our generator starts with a
F_{t}^{-1}
on the left

\pi_{s}^{+}\left(\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\overbrace{F_{t}^{-1}F_{s}^{-1}\ldots}^{k}\right)=\pi_{s}^{+}\left(F_{t}^{-1}\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\overbrace{F_{s}^{-1}\ldots}^{k-1}\right)=F_{t}^{-1}\pi_{s}^{+}\left(\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\overbrace{F_{s}^{-1}\ldots}^{k-1}\right)\simeq 0

where we have used bilinearity of
\pi_{s}^{+}
and the previous case. Similar calculations hold when
m
is even. Thus by
Eq.

8
the image of
\mathcal{U}_{t}^{-}
lands in
\mathcal{U}_{t}^{+}
as desired.

Similarly, tensoring on the left with
\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}^{-1}
sends
\mathcal{U}_{t}^{+}
to
\mathcal{U}_{t}^{-}
and it is clear that these functors are inverse equivalences.
∎

4.2
Relative Serre Duality

Theorem 4.5
.

\pi_{s}^{-}(X^{\bullet})\simeq\pi_{s}^{+}(\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\otimes_{R}X^{\bullet})\simeq\pi_{s}^{+}(X^{\bullet}\otimes_{R}\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t})
for all
X^{\bullet}\in K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m)))
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the first equivalence as the second proceeds similarly. When
m
is odd, we have that

\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}=F^{\bullet}_{(st)^{m}}F_{t}^{-2}\simeq\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{t}}^{m-1}\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{s}}^{m}F_{t}^{-1}\overset{braid}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{s}}^{m-2}F_{t}^{2}\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{s}}^{m-2}

(10)

As in the proof of
4.1
using
Eq.

6
we obtain the distinguished triangle

\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{s}}^{m-2}F_{t}^{2}\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{s}}^{m-2}\to\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{t}}^{m-3}F_{s}^{2}\overbrace{F_{t}\ldots F_{s}}^{m-3}\to\mathrm{Tot}\left(\overbrace{F_{s}F_{t}\ldots}^{2m-3}(-1)\to\overbrace{F_{s}F_{t}\ldots}^{2m-3}(1)\right)\xrightarrow{+1}

Using
4.1

(ii)
the last term is homotopy equivalent to 0 after applying
\pi_{s}^{+}
, so we obtain the following chain of homotopy equivalences

\pi_{s}^{+}\left(\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\right)=\pi_{s}^{+}\left(\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{s}}^{m-2}F_{t}^{2}\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{s}}^{m-2}\right)\simeq\pi_{s}^{+}\left(\overbrace{F_{s}\ldots F_{t}}^{m-3}F_{s}^{2}\overbrace{F_{t}\ldots F_{s}}^{m-3}\right)\simeq\ldots\simeq\pi_{s}^{+}\left(R\right)=R

(11)

Note that it is crucial that the start and ends of the expressions above end in
s
as
\pi_{s}^{+}(F_{s})\simeq 0
by
Corollary

3.9
(ii)
but
\pi_{s}^{+}(F_{t})\not\simeq 0
! When
m
is even,
Eq.

10
has
F_{s}^{2}
in the middle (for the case
m=2
,
\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}=F_{s}^{2}
) but the start and end still consists of
F_{s}
so the same arguments above also show
Eq.

11
in this case.

Using
Corollary

4.3
,
X^{\bullet}
fits into the distinguished triangle

\pi_{s}^{-}(X^{\bullet})\xrightarrow{\eta}X^{\bullet}\to C^{-}(X^{\bullet})\xrightarrow{+1}

where
C^{-}(X^{\bullet})=\mathrm{Cone}(\eta)\in\mathcal{U}^{-}_{t}
. Tensoring with
\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}
on the left gives us the distinguished triangle

\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\otimes_{R}\pi_{s}^{-}(X^{\bullet})\to\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\otimes_{R}X^{\bullet}\to\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\otimes_{R}C^{-}(X^{\bullet})\xrightarrow{+1}

\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\otimes_{R}C^{-}(X^{\bullet})\in\mathcal{U}_{t}^{+}
by
Lemma

4.4
so the last term is homotopy equivalent to 0 after applying the triangulated functor
\pi_{s}^{+}
. Thus by bilinearity and
Eq.

11
we see that

\pi_{s}^{-}(X^{\bullet})\simeq\pi_{s}^{+}\left(\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\right)\otimes_{R}\pi_{s}^{-}(X^{\bullet})=\pi_{s}^{+}\left(\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\otimes_{R}\pi_{s}^{-}(X^{\bullet})\right)=\pi_{s}^{+}\left(\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\otimes_{R}X^{\bullet}\right)

(12)

∎

4.3
Dualities

Lemma 4.6

(
[
EMTW20
, Exercise 18.13]
,
[
Soe07
, Proposition 5.9]
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{h}
be a realization of a Coxeter group
W
and let
B,B^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W)
. Then

\mathbb{D}(B\otimes_{R}B^{\prime})=\mathbb{D}(B)\otimes_{R}\mathbb{D}(B^{\prime})

Let us now introduce two other “duality” functors on
\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W)
and their interactions with
\mathbb{D}
.

Definition 4.7
.

(1)

\omega:\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W)\to\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W)

is the
covariant
graded, anti-monoidal functor which sends
B_{s}\mapsto B_{s}
, fixes morphisms, and satisfies
\omega(MN)=\omega(N)\omega(M)
and
\omega(M(1))=\omega(M)(1)
.

(2)

(-)^{\vee}:\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W)\to\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W)^{op}
is the
contravariant
anti-graded, anti-monoidal functor which sends
B_{s}\mapsto B_{s}
, flips morphisms diagrammatically, and satisfies

(MN)^{\vee}=N^{\vee}M^{\vee}
and
M(1)^{\vee}=M^{\vee}(-1).

Because
\mathbb{D}(B_{s})\cong B_{s}

[
EMTW20
, Exercise 18.12]
, it follows from
Lemma

4.6
that

\omega(\mathbb{D}(-))=(-)^{\vee}=\mathbb{D}(\omega(-))

(13)

The monoidal or anti-monoidality of these 3 functors extend to
K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W))
as well as
Eq.

13
. They are also all fully faithful and because
\mathrm{HH}^{k}(MN)\overset{v.s.}{\cong}\mathrm{HH}^{k}(NM)
it follows that
\mathrm{HH}^{k}\circ\omega\overset{v.s.}{\cong}\mathrm{HH}^{k}
and thus

\mathrm{HH}^{k}(\mathbb{D}(X^{\bullet}))\overset{v.s.}{\cong}\mathrm{HH}^{k}\left(\left(X^{\bullet}\right)^{\vee}\right)

but not as
R-
modules! Because
B_{s}
is a Frobenius algebra object in
\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W)
(see
[
EMTW20
, Section 8.2]
), the functor
B_{s}\otimes_{R}(-)
is self adjoint. Likewise with
(-)\otimes_{R}B_{s}
from which it follows that for all
B\in\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W)

\mathrm{Hom}_{R^{e}}(B\otimes_{R}M,N)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{R^{e}}(M,B^{\vee}\otimes_{R}N)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{Hom}_{R^{e}}(M\otimes_{R}B,N)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{R^{e}}(M,N\otimes_{R}B^{\vee})

As a result, for any
X^{\bullet}\in K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W))

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R^{e}}(X^{\bullet},Y^{\bullet})\overset{\text{right }R}{\cong}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R^{e}}(R,(X^{\bullet})^{\vee}\otimes_{R}Y^{\bullet})\overset{\text{right }R}{\cong}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R^{e}}((Y^{\bullet})^{\vee}\otimes_{R}X^{\bullet},R)

4.3.1
Final Computation

Lemma 4.8
.

Given
B\in\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m))
,
\mathrm{HH}^{0}(B)=\pi^{-}_{t}\circ\pi^{-}_{s}(B)
and
\mathrm{HH}^{0}(B)=\pi^{+}_{t}\circ\pi^{+}_{s}(B)
.

Proof.

As vector spaces we have that
V^{*}=\mathbbm{k}\rho_{s}\oplus\mathbbm{k}\rho_{t}\oplus(V^{*})^{s,t}
. Let
\left\{e_{1}^{*},\ldots,e_{r}^{*}\right\}
be a basis for
(V^{*})^{s,t}
. Then using the center and co-center interpretation of
\mathrm{HH}^{0}
and
\mathrm{HH}_{0}
, respectively, we see that

\mathrm{HH}^{0}(B)=\pi^{-}_{t}\circ\pi^{-}_{s}\circ\pi^{-}_{e_{1}^{*}}\ldots\circ\pi^{-}_{e_{r}^{*}},\qquad\mathrm{HH}_{0}(B)=\pi^{+}_{t}\circ\pi^{+}_{s}\circ\pi^{+}_{e_{1}^{*}}\ldots\circ\pi^{+}_{e_{r}^{*}}

However,
\pi^{-}_{e_{i}^{*}}(B)\cong\pi^{+}_{e_{i}^{*}}(B)\cong B
for all
B\in\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m))
and
1\leq i\leq r
and thus the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 4.9
.

\pi^{-}_{t},\pi^{+}_{t}
are the right and left adjoints to the inclusion functor
\left\langle\boxed{R}\right\rangle\to K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},\left\langle t\right\rangle))
and

\pi_{t}^{-}(Z^{\bullet})\simeq\pi_{s}^{+}\left(F_{t}^{2}\otimes_{R}Z^{\bullet}\right)\simeq\pi_{s}^{+}\left(Z^{\bullet}\otimes_{R}F_{t}^{2}\right)\qquad\forall Z^{\bullet}\in K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},\left\langle t\right\rangle))

(14)

Proof.

The first statement follows from
Theorem

3.3
while
Eq.

14
follows from the same proof as in
Theorem

4.5
where we instead have
\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\longleftrightarrow F_{t}^{2}
,
\mathcal{U}^{-}_{t}\longleftrightarrow\text{span of }\left\{F_{t}^{-1}\right\}
, and
\mathcal{U}^{+}_{t}\longleftrightarrow\text{span of}\left\{F_{t}\right\}
.
∎

Remark
.

t
and
s
can be switched in the above two lemmas.

Theorem 4.10

(Serre Duality)
.

For any two complexes
X^{\bullet},Y^{\bullet}\in K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m)))
one has the following homotopy equivalences of complexes of right (or left)
R-
modules.

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R^{e}}(X^{\bullet},Y^{\bullet})\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R^{e}}(Y^{\bullet},\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}}^{-1}\otimes_{R}X^{\bullet})^{\vee}\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R^{e}}(\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}}\otimes_{R}Y^{\bullet},X^{\bullet})^{\vee}

Proof.

Let
B\in\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m))
. From the universal coefficient spectral sequence we have that

\mathrm{Ext}_{R}^{p}(\mathrm{HH}_{q}(B),R)\implies\mathrm{Ext}_{R^{e}}^{p+q}(R,\mathrm{Hom}_{R}(B,R))

But by
Lemma

2.5
,
\mathrm{HH}^{q}(B)
and thus
\mathrm{HH}_{q}(B)
(see
[
GHMN19
, Lemma 3.4]
) is a free
R
module and thus

\mathrm{HH}_{q}(B)^{\vee}=\mathbb{D}(\mathrm{HH}_{q}(B))\cong\mathrm{HH}^{q}(\mathbb{D}(B))\qquad\qquad\forall B\in\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},I_{2}(m))

(15)

where the first equality is because
\omega=\mathrm{id}
on complexes of
R-
modules. Now we compute

\displaystyle\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R^{e}}(X^{\bullet},Y^{\bullet})

\displaystyle\cong\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R^{e}}(R,(X^{\bullet})^{\vee}\otimes_{R}Y^{\bullet})=\mathrm{HH}^{0}((X^{\bullet})^{\vee}\otimes_{R}Y^{\bullet})=\pi_{t}^{-}\circ\pi_{s}^{-}((X^{\bullet})^{\vee}\otimes_{R}Y^{\bullet})

\displaystyle\overset{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{relsereq}}{\simeq}\pi_{t}^{-}\pi_{s}^{+}\left(\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\otimes_{R}(X^{\bullet})^{\vee}\otimes_{R}Y^{\bullet}\right)\overset{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{rank1releq}}{\simeq}\pi_{t}^{+}\left(F_{t}^{2}\pi_{s}^{+}\left(\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}\otimes_{R}(X^{\bullet})^{\vee}\otimes_{R}Y^{\bullet}\right)\right)

By
Lemma

3.6
, we can pull
F_{t}^{2}
inside
\pi_{s}^{+}
and thus the RHS equals

\displaystyle\mathrm{HH}_{0}\left(\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}}\otimes_{R}(X^{\bullet})^{\vee}\otimes_{R}Y^{\bullet}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{dhheq}}{\cong}\mathrm{HH}^{0}\left(\mathbb{D}\left(\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}}\otimes_{R}(X^{\bullet})^{\vee}\otimes_{R}Y^{\bullet}\right)\right)^{\vee}\overset{}{\cong}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R^{e}}\left(R,\mathbb{D}\left(\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}})\otimes_{R}\mathbb{D}((X^{\bullet})^{\vee})\otimes_{R}\mathbb{D}(Y^{\bullet})\right)\right)^{\vee}

\displaystyle\cong\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R^{e}}\left(\mathbb{D}(Y^{\bullet})^{\vee},\mathbb{D}\left(\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}})\otimes_{R}\mathbb{D}((X^{\bullet})^{\vee})\right)\right)^{\vee}\overset{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{wdveq}}{\cong}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R^{e}}\left(\omega(Y^{\bullet}),\omega\left(\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}}^{-1}\right)\otimes_{R}\omega(X^{\bullet})\right)^{\vee}

\displaystyle\cong\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R^{e}}\left(\omega(Y^{\bullet}),\omega\left(\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}^{-1}\otimes_{R}\omega(X^{\bullet})\right)\right)\cong\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R^{e}}\left(Y^{\bullet},\mathrm{FT}_{\left\{s,t\right\}/t}^{-1}\otimes_{R}\omega(X^{\bullet})\right)^{\vee}

∎

5
Remarks about the General Case

Given a general Coxeter system
(W,S)
and a parabolic subgroup
W_{I}
, where
I\subset S
, it was proven in
[
GHMN19
, Appendix A]
that the left and right adjoints to the natural inclusion functor
K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W_{I}))\to K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W))
exists. We conjecture that they have an explicit form, namely

Conjecture 2
.

Let
\mathfrak{h}
be a realization of
W
with fundamental weights
\left\{\rho_{s}\right\}_{s\in S}
. Given a subset
I\subset S
, let
\left\{s_{1},\ldots,s_{\ell}\right\}
denote the complement of
I
in
S
. Let
\pi_{L},\pi_{R}:K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W))\to K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W_{I}))
be the left and right adjoints to the inclusion functor
K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W_{I}))\to K^{b}(\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W))
. Then

\pi_{L}\cong\pi^{+}_{s_{1}}\circ\ldots\circ\pi^{+}_{s_{\ell}},\qquad\qquad\pi_{R}\cong\pi^{-}_{s_{1}}\circ\ldots\circ\pi^{-}_{s_{\ell}}

This is independent of the order chosen for the complement of
I
in
S
.

Remark
.

The functors used in the proof of
1
in Type A for the special case of subgroups
S_{r}\times(S_{1})^{n-r}\subset S_{n}
do not fit the framework above because
\left\{x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}\right\}
are not fundamental weights for the
\mathbb{C}^{n}
realization of
S_{n}
. However,
x_{n}
is a fundamental weight. Thus, for the corresponding subgroup
S_{r}\times(S_{1})^{n-r}
, the composition

\pi^{\pm}=\pi^{\pm}_{x_{r+1}}\circ\ldots\circ\pi^{\pm}_{x_{n}}

in this order gives the correct adjoints, but using these functors for the other parabolic subgroups of
S_{n}
will fail.

One can show that it suffices to consider the maximal parabolic case
W\setminus\left\{s\right\}\subset W
for
2
. Similar to
Corollary

3.4
,
2
follows from showing that
\pi^{\pm}_{s}
sends Soergel Bimodules to Soergel Bimodules, namely

\pi^{\pm}_{s}(B_{w})\in\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}(\mathfrak{h},W_{S\setminus\left\{s\right\}})\quad\forall w\in W

(16)

If one works with the homotopy category, it also suffices to show
\pi^{\pm}_{s}
sends Bott-Samelsons to Soergel Bimodules. For the maximal parabolic
S_{n-1}\times S_{1}\subset S_{n}
considered in
[
GHMN19
]
,
Eq.

16
easily follows from the fact that any
w\in S_{n}\setminus(S_{n-1}\times S_{1})
can be written as
w=us_{n-1}v
where

u,v\in S_{n-1}\times S_{1}
. Another easy consequence of this fact is

\ker\pi^{+}_{s_{n-1}}=\left\langle F_{w}|w\in W,\,w\not\in S_{n-1}\times S_{1}\right\rangle\qquad\ker\pi^{-}_{s_{n-1}}=\left\langle F_{w}^{-1}|w\in W,\,w\not\in S_{n-1}\times S_{1}\right\rangle

(17)

For other maximal parabolics
4
4

4

even in Type
A
.
of a Coxeter group, this fact is not true as the double coset space has size greater than 2. We overcome this obstacle in this paper by employing the tools developed in
[
Li25a
]
, arising from the diagrammatic presentation of Ext-enhanced Soergel bimodules in rank 2. We expect that a diagrammatic presentation of Ext-enhanced Soergel bimodules for arbitrary Coxeter groups
W
will yield
Eq.

16
and the analogous
Eq.

17
for
W_{I}\subset W
, from which
2
and
1
will readily follow.

6
Computations of Triply-graded link homology

Proposition 6.1

(Gaussian Elimination
[
Eli18
, Proposition 5.16]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an additive category. Suppose that
C^{\bullet}\in C(\mathcal{A})
has the form

\ldots\to C^{k-1}\xrightarrow{\begin{pmatrix}b\\
a\end{pmatrix}}X\oplus D^{k}\xrightarrow{\begin{pmatrix}\psi&d\\
e&c\end{pmatrix}}Y\oplus D^{k+1}\xrightarrow{\begin{pmatrix}g&f\\
\end{pmatrix}}C^{k+2}

(18)

where
\psi:X\to Y
is an isomorphism. Then
C^{\bullet}\simeq\widetilde{C^{\bullet}}
in
K(\mathcal{A})
where
\widetilde{C^{\bullet}}
is the complex

\displaystyle\ldots\to C^{k-1}\xrightarrow{a}D^{k}\xrightarrow{c-e\psi^{-1}d}D^{k+1}\xrightarrow{f}C^{k+2}\to\ldots

with projection

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\oplus D^{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\oplus D^{k+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{k+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0\ 1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-e\psi^{-1}\ 1)}

and inclusion

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{k+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\oplus D^{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\oplus D^{k+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{bmatrix}-\psi^{-1}d\\
1\end{bmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{bmatrix}0\\
1\end{bmatrix}}
.

Corollary 6.2
.

In the above proposition, if
d
or
e=0
(for example if
D^{k}=0
or
D^{k+1}=0
) we can remove
X
and
Y
without worrying about any changes to the differential.

Now, because the RHS of
Eq.

19
has many contractible summands, we can remove them to yield the complex
R(1)\xrightarrow{\alpha_{s}+\alpha_{t}}\boxed{R(3)}
. Since
\pi_{s}^{-}(F_{s}^{-2})\simeq\boxed{R}
, it follows that

\pi_{s}^{-}(\pi_{t}^{+}(W^{\bullet}))\simeq R(1)\xrightarrow{\alpha_{s}+\alpha_{t}}\boxed{R(3)}

The nonzero cohomology of
\pi_{s}^{+}(\pi_{t}^{-}(W^{\bullet}))
will then be

\mathrm{H}^{T=0}(\pi_{s}^{-}(\pi_{t}^{+}(W^{\bullet})))=\frac{R(3)}{(\alpha_{s}+\alpha_{t})}

It follows that the nonzero cohomology at
A=1
will be

\left(\overline{\mathrm{HHH}}^{A=1}\right)^{T=-1}\cong\mathbbm{k}(1),\qquad\left(\overline{\mathrm{HHH}}^{A=1}\right)^{T=0}\cong\mathbbm{k}(3)\oplus\frac{R(3)}{(\alpha_{s}+\alpha_{t})},\quad\left(\overline{\mathrm{HHH}}^{A=1}\right)^{T=1}\cong\mathbbm{k}(5),\quad\left(\overline{\mathrm{HHH}}^{A=1}\right)^{T=2}\cong\mathbbm{k}(7)

The total Poincare series for
\overline{\mathrm{HHH}}(F_{s}^{-2}F_{t}F_{s}^{-1}F_{t})
will then be

\displaystyle\overline{\mathscr{P}(A,Q,T)}(F_{s}^{-2}F_{t}F_{s}^{-1}F_{t})

\displaystyle=TQ^{-1}+T^{2}Q^{-3}+A\left(T^{-1}Q^{-1}+Q^{-3}+\frac{Q^{-3}}{(1-Q^{2})}+TQ^{-5}+T^{2}Q^{-7}\right)+A^{2}\left(T^{-1}Q^{-5}+\frac{Q^{-7}}{(1-Q^{2})}\right)

Substituting
A=-a^{2}q^{2},Q=q,T=-1
into
\overline{\mathscr{P}(A,Q,T)}(F_{s}^{-2}F_{t}F_{s}^{-1}F_{t})
and multiplying by the unit
q^{2}a^{-3}
, one readily verifies that this coincides with the HOMFLY–PT polynomial of
L5a1
computed in
[
LM25
]

\frac{1}{vz}-\frac{v}{z}-\frac{z}{v^{3}}+\frac{2z}{v}-vz+\frac{z^{3}}{v}

after setting
z=q-q^{-1}
and
v=a
.

Warning
.

Invalid format of field ’month’
